A. CONSERVATION OF ENERGY: 
 Time Technoplas t is deeply commi ed to sustainable business pracces aimed at protecng the environmen t. Ener gy 
conser vaon is a corner stone of our eﬀorts to preser ve natural resour ces. As a responsible corpor ate enty, we undert ake

moderniz aon.
 Aligned with our commitmen t to comba t clima te chang e and limit global warming , we are acv ely working towards achieving 
carbon neutr ality and net zero greenhouse gas emissions across all scopes. Eﬀecv e ener gy manag emen t is a core elemen t of 
our oper aons.
 Our manuf acturing units are dedic ated to reducing ener gy consumpon, and we have implemen ted several steps in this 
regard:
 i. Installaon of Solar Panels:  We have installed solar panels at various plan ts to harness alterna ve sour ces of ener gy and 
reduce electricity consumpon.
 ii. Rain water Harvesng: We have set up systems to harvest rainwater and reuse it within our oper aons, contribung to 
water conser vaon eﬀorts.
 iii. Power Quality Audit: We regularly conduct pow er quality audits at our plan ts to assess actual uliz aon and iden fy any 
wastage, allowing us to opmiz e ener gy usag e.

technology and connually upgr ading our equipmen t to impr ove ener gy performance.
  Recognising ener gy consumpon’ s signiﬁc ant role in greenhouse gas emissions and oper aonal costs, we priorise 
enhancing energy eﬃciency. We connuously reﬁne energy managem ent system s, emphasising opmal asset 
ulisa on, best pracces adopon and ener gy­eﬃcien t technology integraon.

 OVERVIEW OF ECONOMY
 Global Econom y
 The 2024 World Economic Outlook (WEO) from the IMF, projects global growth to remain steady at appr oxima tely 3.2 percent 
for both 2024 and 2025. Median headline inﬂa on is expect ed to decr ease from 2.8 percent during 2024 to 2.4 percent in 
2025, suggesng a gradual stabiliz aon of price levels. Furthermor e, global inﬂa on is ancipa ted to decline from 6.8 percent 
in 2023 to 5.9 percent in 2024, and further to 4.5 percent in 2025.
 IMF has noted a slow down in global disinﬂa on momen tum, suggesng a higher likelihood of extended elevated interest 
rates, which c ould incr ease e xternal, ﬁsc al, and ﬁnancial risk s. Adv anced ec onomies ar e project ed to see a sligh t incr ease in 
growth, rising from 1.6 percent in 2023 to 1.7 percent in 2024 and 1.8 percent in 2025. Mean while, growth in emer ging 
mark ets and developing economies is expect ed to moder ate sligh tly, from 4.3 percent in 2023 to 4.2 percent in both 2024 and 
2025.
 Fiscal policies may need to focus on adjus ng for inﬂa on, addr essing rising debt pressur es, and implemen ng structur al 
reforms to enhance human capital and leverage technologic al advancemen ts. These measur es are crucial for driving futur e 
output and producvity growth. Addionally , supporng vulner able popula ons and impr oving supply ­side condions will be 
essen al for achieving ﬁscal consolida on and smoother inﬂa on adjus tmen ts toward targeted levels.Bo om of Form
 Indian Econom y
 India’ s economic outlook remains robus t, with projecons indic ang real GDP growth between 6.5–7 percent for FY 2024­25. 
The econom y has emer ged swily from the blow s of the pandemic as real GDP in FY24 was up 20% as compar ed to FY20. The 
Reser ve Bank of India (RBI) has revised its growth forecast upw ard for 2024/25 to 7.2 percent, cing a resur gence in private 
consumpon, robus t investmen t, and increased exports. Similarly , the IMF has increased India’ s growth forecast to 7 percent 
for 2024­2025, up from 6.8 percent, aligning with upda tes from other rang agencies and economis ts. This growth traject ory 
under scores India’ s posion as a leading alterna ve to China for global supply chain diversiﬁcaon, parcularly amid strained 
US­China relaons.
 Aa per World Economic Outlook (WEO) report issued by IMF, India’ s growth forecast is strongly main tained at 6.8 percent in 
2024 and 6.5 percent in 2025, driven by sustained domes c demand and a growing working­ag e popula on. The country’s 
ability to mig ate the eﬀects of the global economic slow down through robus t governmen t capital expenditur e, resilien t 
domes c demand, and other economic buﬀers has reinforced its economic resilience. This has can be show cased as, India has 
success fully manag ed to keep retail inﬂa on at 5.4% in FY 2023­24, the lowest level since the COVID­19 pandemic.
 India’ s strong fundamen tals, including health y balance sheets for bank s and corpor ates, ﬁscal consolida on eﬀorts, 
manag eable external balances, and subs tanal foreign exchang e reser ves, contribut e to a posiv e economic outlook. These 
factors collecv ely support India’ s connued economic momen tum and its role as a key player in the global econom y.

 KEY RISKS
 Raw material availability
 We have not faced signiﬁc ant challeng es in sour cing our primary raw materials. PE granules, which are produced from oil and 
natural gas, are essen al across all our business divisions. Mos t of these granules are import ed from neighbouring countries, 
with the rest obtained from local manuf actur ers. Our procur emen t strategy includes fulﬁlling mos t of our needs through open 
mark et pur chases or both short ­term and long­t erm supply c ontracts. As the global mark et for recycled plas cs ev olves, w e 
ancipa te incr easing demand f or vir gin poly ethylene. Coun tries such as China, India, Vie tnam, Indonesia, the Unit ed St ates, 
and those in Europe are signiﬁc antly invesng in recycling , which is expect ed to sustain overall demand in the long term.
 Commodity price risk
 The Compan y is exposed to ﬂuctua ons in polymer prices which are determined by the supply and demand in the Indian and 
interna onal mark ets. Since polymer s are crude deriv aves, the prices also tend to follow crude prices which are volale, and 
this volality inﬂuences Compan y’s income and net proﬁt.
 Foreign exchang e and other risk
 Oper ang in interna onal mark ets presen ts us with risks that diﬀer from those in India. These include currency ﬂuctua ons, 
import/ export regula ons, customs procedur es, and chang es in governmen t policies and regula ons oen found in 
developing countries. Addionally , we face challeng es such as labour unrest, geopolic al instability , conﬂict, terrorism, 
defaults in certain jurisdicons, and hyperinﬂa on. Paymen ts from our overseas subsidiaries can be impact ed by restricons 
on currency conversion to US dollar s, chang es in tax policies, and other trade compliance regula ons.
 HUMAN RESOURCES OVERVIEW
 Connuous learning is a core aspect of our compan y cultur e. Every employ ee, regardless of their posion, recogniz es the 
import ance of ongoing developmen t and skill enhancemen t. A commitmen t to learning is a fundamen tal requir emen t for 
being part of our organizaon. We aim to support and inspir e our employ ees by oﬀering aracv e and feasible career 
opportunies that allow them to develop their skills over me, adapng to economic chang es and a dynamic environmen t.
 Managing indus trial relaons is the direct responsibility of local manag emen t. This will be addr essed ﬁrst at the site level 
(factories, warehouses) and, if necessary , escalated to the regional or naonal level, in line with local laws and pracces.
 ENVIRONMENT HEALTH AND SAFETY
 TIME TECHNOPLAST LTD places a high priority on the health and safety of everyone involved in its oper aons, as well as on 
main taining a sustainable oper ang environmen t. Employ ees at all levels work together to impr ove environmen tal, 
occupa onal health, and safety standar ds, aiming to exceed established benchmark s. The compan y’s EHS policy not only 
adher es to all relevant legal requir emen ts but also focuses on mov ang employ ees, as well as on their learning and training. 
External audits are performed to assess the eﬀecv eness of the EHS policy and inia ves, and the recommenda ons from 
these audits are consider ed for futur e impr ovemen ts.

2. Corpor ate Governance Structur e
 The Corpor ate Governance structur e of the Compan y is as follow s: 
 Boar d of Directors: The Boar d is entrusted with an ulma te responsibility of the manag emen t, direcons and performance of 
the Compan y. As its primary role is ﬁduciary in nature, the Boar d provides leader ship, strategic guidance, objecv e and 
independen t view to the Compan y’s manag emen t while dischar ging its responsibilies, thus ensuring that the manag emen t 
adher es to ethics, transpar ency and disclosur es. 
 Commi ees of the Boar d: The Boar d Commi ees are established by the Boar d and are governed by its terms of reference, 
which outline the scope, composion, tenur e, funconing , and reporng parame ters. These Commi ees play a pivotal role in 
the governance structur e of the Compan y, being established to addr ess speciﬁc areas of concern that requir e more detailed 
review . They oper ate under the direct super vision of the Boar d and Chairper sons of the respecv e Commi ees then debrie f 
the Boar d with upda tes on the deliber aons and decisions made by the Commi ees. 
 Chairman: The primary role of the Chairman is to provide leader ship to the Boar d in achieving goals of the Compan y. He is 
responsible, inter­alia, for the working of the Boar d and for ensuring that all relevant issues are placed before the Boar d and 
that all Directors are encouraged to provide their expert guidance on the relevant issues raised in the mee ngs of the Boar d. 
He is also responsible for formula ng the corpor ate strategy along with other member s of the Boar d. 
 Execuv e Directors: Execuv e directors are individuals who hold senior manag emen t posions within a compan y and are 
tasked with making cric al strategic decisions. They are oen deeply involved in the daily oper aons of the business and play a 
pivotal role in spearheading the compan y’s growth and proﬁtability . Execuv e directors are typic ally appoin ted by the boar d of 
directors and are held accountable for the overall performance of the organizaon. 
 Non­Ex ecuv e Directors including Independen t Directors: Non­Ex ecuv e Directors bring a diverse range of skills and 
perspecv es to the boar droom, helping to ensur e that all decisions are thor oughly consider ed and in the best interest of the 
compan y. Their independence from manag emen t allow s them to provide unbiased oversight and challeng e, promong 
transpar ency and accountability within the organizaon. Non­Ex ecuv e Directors also play a crucial role in seng the 
compan y’s strategic direcon and monit oring its performance against key objecv es. CORPORATE GOVERNANCE REPORT

the shar eholder s should open a Dema t account with a Deposit ory 
Parcipan t (DP). The shar eholder is requir ed to ﬁll in a Dema t 
Reques t Form and submit the same along with the original shar e 
cerﬁc ates to his DP. The DP will alloc ate a dema t reques t number 
and shall forward the reques t physically and electr onic ally through 
NSDL/CDSL to Registrar & Transfer Agent. On receipt of the dema t 
reques t both physically and electr onic ally and aer veriﬁc aon, the 
shar es are dema terializ ed and an electr onic credit of the shar es is 
given in the account of the shar eholder .TIME TECHNOPLAST LIMITED

8. TRANSFER OF UNCLAIMED / UNPAID AMOUNTS TO THE INVESTOR EDUCATION AND PROTECTION FUND (IEPF):
 In accordance with the provisions of Secons 124, 125 and other applic able provisions, if any, of the Act, read with the IEPF 
Authority (Acc ounng, Audit, Transfer and Refund) Rules, 2016 (hereina er referred to as “IEPF Rules”) (including any 
statutory modiﬁc aon(s) or re­enactmen t(s) thereof for the me being in force), the amoun t of dividend remaining unclaimed 
or unpaid for a period of seven years from the date of transfer to the unpaid dividend account is requir ed to be transferred to 
the IEPF, main tained by the Central Governmen t. In pursuance of this, the dividend remaining unclaimed or unpaid in respect 
of dividends declar ed upto the ﬁnancial year ended March 31, 2016 have been transferred to the IEPF. The details of the 
unclaimed dividends so transferred are available on the Compan y’s websit e at  and on the websit e www.me technoplas t.com
of the Minis try of Corpor ate Aﬀairs at www.mca.gov.in.
 It may be noted that the unclaimed dividend for the ﬁnancial year 2016­17 declar ed on Sept ember 29, 2017 is due to be 
transferred to the IEPF by October 27, 2024. The same can, how ever, be claimed by the Member s by Sept ember 28, 2024.

claims to the Compan y’s Registrar and Shar e Transfer Agents before they are due to be transferred to the IEPF. 
 In accordance with Secon 124(6) of the Act, read with the IEPF Rules, all the shar es in respect of which dividend has remained 
unclaimed or unpaid for seven consecuv e years or more are requir ed to be transferred to the dema t Account of the IEPF 
Authority . Accordingly , all the shar es in respect of which dividends were declar ed upto the ﬁnancial years ended March 31, 
2016 and remained unpaid or unclaimed have been transferred to the IEPF. The Compan y had sent noces to all such 
member s in this r egard and published a new spaper adv ersemen t and ther eaer tr ansferred the shar es to the IEPF during 
ﬁnancial year 2023­24. The details of such shares transferred have been uploaded in the Company’s website 
www.me technoplas t.com.

IEPF Authority aer complying with the procedur e prescribed under the IEPF Rules. The Member/ Claiman t is requir ed to 
mak e an online applic aon to the IEPF Authority in Form No. IEPF­5 (available on iepf.gov.in) along with requisit e fees as 
decided by the IEPF Authority from me to me. The Member/Claiman t can ﬁle only one consolida ted claim in a ﬁnancial year 
as per the IEPF Rules.

At Time Technoplas t Limit ed we are commi ed to integrang sustainable pracces into every aspect of our oper aons, aligning with 
our core values and strategic objecv es. 
We are proud to be the ﬁrst Compan y in India to receiv e ﬁnal appr oval for the manuf acturing and supply of Type­ IV Composit e 
Cylinder s for hydrogen. The technology of hydrogen cylinder represen ts a transforma ve step in reducing carbon emissions and 
promong sustainable green ener gy soluons. Our eﬀorts in this area demons trate our dedic aon to pioneering sustainable 
innov aons that contribut e to a greener futur e. We believ e that sustainable business pracces, including our advancemen ts in 
hydrogen cylinder technology , not only contribut e to the well­being of our plane t and socie ty but also enhance our compe veness 
and create value for our stakeholders. This Business Responsibility & Sustainability Report (BRSR) outlines our eﬀorts, 
achiev emen ts, and futur e goals in our journe y towards a more sustainable and responsible business model.

3. Describe the processes in place to safely reclaim your products for reusing , recycling , and disposing at the end of life, for (a) Plascs 
(including pack aging) (b) E­waste (c) Hazardous waste, and (d) other waste.
 The plasc materials are reused in accordance with the regula tory and standar d norms of the State/Coun try. In certain instances, the waste is 
either sold t o registered vendor s or tr ansport ed to authoriz ed recyclers. Internally g ener ated ma terials ar e grinded and r eused in a manner 
that ensur es opmal quan ty without compr omising quality .
 (a) Plasc waste ­ For Packaging products, as per Plasc Waste Manag emen t Rules, 2016, Compan y had reprocessed the material through 
authoriz ed re­processor aer consumer use.
 (b) E­waste – Compan y is gener ang E­waste like DG set baeries as per the requir emen t and disposed the same through manuf actur er on 
buy back basis for other gener al E­waste disposed through authoriz ed re­processor .
 (c) Hazardous waste – Compan y is gener ang following waste:
Sr. No. Material Disposal Method
1 Used Oil Disposed to Authoriz ed re­processor
2 Used Coon Waste Disposed to CHWTSDF
3 Used Empty inks boles, container s (1­2 Litre) Disposed to CHWTSDF
 Other examples of hazardous waste include explosiv es, toxic or ﬂammable gases, ﬂammable liquids and solids, oxidizing subs tances, 
radioacv e materials, and corrosive subs tances. The Compan y has designa ted a hazardous waste storage pad equipped with ﬁre­ﬁghng 
arrangemen ts and secondary containmen t. All hazardous waste in solid form is stored in labeled drums as per Form­8 regula ons. Workers are 
trained in hazardous waste handling and provided with appr opria te PPE. The Compan y is a member of a local authoriz ed party for the 
treatmen t and disposal of hazardous waste, and the Compan y also reuses certain wastes wher e possible.

10. Health and Safety Manag emen t System
 a. Whe ther an occupa onal health and safety manag emen t system has been implemen ted by the enty? (Yes/ No). If yes, the coverage 
such system?
  Yes, the Compan y has an Integrated Manag emen t System meeng the requir emen ts speciﬁed in ISO 45001:2018 Occupa onal Health 
and Safety Manag emen t System. The Compan y is cerﬁed by TUV. The Compan y conducts safety programs at all sites to ensur e safety of 
all the labour ers/workers on site. Safety shoe, jackets, helmets, gloves are manda tory for onsit e workforce ­ periodic safety assessmen t 
are undert aken to ensur e compliance
 b. Wha t are the processes used to iden fy work­related hazards and assess risks on a roune and non­r oune basis by the enty?
  Our Plan ts have already obtained ISO 45001:2018 cerﬁc aon and we have manda ted to obtain said cerﬁc ate for every new plan t at 
the start of every Project and at the start of every acvity . The Compan y has a process of Hazard Iden ﬁcaon and Risk Assessmen t 
wher ein the Safety and Execuon team together iden fy the hazards associa ted with these acvies and evalua tes the risks vis a vis the 
control measur es in place. This is done for all roune and non­r oune acvies. The Compan y also has health & safety professionals on 
site to regularly keep a check on the acvies carried out and a regular check of all the safety equipmen t’s takes places.
 c. Whe ther you have processes for workers to report the work­related hazards and to remov e themselv es from such risks. (Y/N)

Manag emen t’s Responsibility for Internal Financial Controls
2.  The Compan y’s Boar d of Directors is responsible for establishing and main taining internal ﬁnancial controls based on the 
internal control over ﬁnancial reporng criteria established by the Compan y considering the essen al componen ts of internal 
control stated in the Guidance Note on Audit of Internal Financial Controls over Financial Reporng issued by the Instut e of 
Chart ered Accountants of India (ICAI). These responsibilies include the design, implemen taon and main tenance of 
adequa te internal ﬁnancial controls that were oper ang eﬀecv ely for ensuring the orderly and eﬃcien t conduct of its 
business, including adher ence to compan y’s policies, the safeguar ding of its asse ts, the prevenon and detecon of frauds and 
errors, the accur acy and comple teness of the accounng records, and the mely prepar aon of reliable ﬁnancial informa on, 
as requir ed under the Act.
Audit ors’ Responsibility
3. Our responsibility is to express an opinion on the Compan y’s IFCoFR based on our audit. We conduct ed our audit in 
accordance with the Standar ds on Auding , issued by the ICAI and deemed to be prescribed under Secon 143(10) of the Act, 
to the extent applic able to an audit of IFCoFR and the Guidance Note issued by the I CAI. Those Standar ds and the Guidance 
Note requir e that we comply with ethical requir emen ts and plan and perform the audit to obtain reasonable assur ance about 
whe ther adequa te IFCoFR were established and main tained and if such controls oper ated eﬀecv ely in all material respects.
4.  Our audit involves performing procedur es to obtain audit evidence about the adequacy of the IFCoFR and their oper ang 
eﬀecv eness. Our audit of IFCoFR included obtaining an under standing of IFCoFR, assessing the risk that a material weakness 
exists, and t esng and ev alua ng the design and oper ang e ﬀecv eness of in ternal c ontrol based on the assessed risk. The 
procedur es select ed depend on the audit or’s judg emen t, including the assessmen t of the risks of material miss tatemen t of the 
ﬁnancial statemen ts, whe ther due to fraud or error.
5.  We believ e that the audit evidence we have obtained is suﬃcien t and appr opria te to provide a basis for our audit opinion on 
the Compan y’s  IFCoFR.
Meaning of Internal Financial Controls Over Financial Reporng
6.  A compan y’s IFCoFR is a process designed to provide reasonable assur ance regarding the reliability of ﬁnancial reporng and 
the prepar aon of ﬁnancial statemen ts for external purposes in accordance with gener ally accept ed accounng principles. A 
compan y’s IFCoFR includes those policies and procedur es that
 (1)  pertain to the main tenance of records that, in reasonable detail, accur ately and fairly reﬂect the transacons and  
disposions of the asse ts of the compan y;
 (2)  provide reasonable assur ance that transacons are recorded as necessary to permit prepar aon of ﬁnancial statemen ts 
in accordance with gener ally accept ed accounng principles, and that receipts and expenditur es of the compan y are 
being made only in accordance with authorisa ons of manag emen t and directors of the compan y; and
 (3)  provide reasonable assur ance regarding prevenon or mely detecon of unauthorised acquision, use, or disposion 
of the compan y’s asse ts that could have a material eﬀect on the ﬁnancial statemen ts.TIME TECHNOPLAST LIMITED Standalone

Inher ent Limit aons of Internal Financial Controls Over Financial Reporng

the IFCoFR to futur e periods are subject to the risk that IFCoFR may become inadequa te because of chang es in condions, or 
that the degr ee of compliance with the policies or procedur es may deterior ate.

Note 1­STATEMENT OF SIGNIFICANT ACCOUNTING POLICIES:
I. Backgr ound
 Time Technoplas t Ltd (TTL or the compan y) incorpor ated in India is a mulnaonal conglomer ate involved in the 
manuf acturing of technology and innov aon driven polymer & Composit e products.  The Compan y is a public limit ed compan y 
and is listed on the Bomba y Stock Exchang e (BSE) and Naonal Stock Exchang e (NSE). 
 II. Signiﬁc ant Accoun ng Policies follow ed by the Compan y
 (a) Basis of Prepar aon
  i) Compliance with Ind AS
   These ﬁnancial statemen ts have been prepar ed in accordance with the Indian Accounng Standar ds (hereina er 
referred to as the ‘Ind AS’) as noﬁed by Minis try of Corpor ate Aﬀairs pursuan t to Secon 133 of the Companies Act, 
2013 (‘Act’) read with of the Companies (Indian Accounng Standar ds) Rules,2015 as amended and other relevant 
provisions of the Act.
   The accounng policies are applied consis tently to all the periods presen ted in the ﬁnancial statemen ts.
  ii) Historical cost convenon
   The ﬁnancial statemen ts have been prepar ed on a historical cost basis, except for the following which have been 
measur ed at fair value :
   1. Cert ain ﬁnancial asse ts and liabilies are measur ed at fair value;
   2. Deﬁned bene ﬁt plans;
   3. Equity seled Shar e Based Paymen ts 
  iii) Curr ent & non current classiﬁc aon
   All asse ts and liabilies have been classiﬁed as current or non­curr ent as per the Compan y’s normal oper ang cycle 
(twelve mon ths) and other criteria set out in the Schedule III to the Act
  iv) Rounding of amoun ts
   All amoun ts disclosed in the ﬁnancial statemen ts and notes have been rounded oﬀ to the near est lakhs as per the 
requir emen t of  Schedule III, unless otherwise stated.
 (b) Use of esma tes and judgmen ts 
  The esma tes and judgmen ts used in the prepar aon of the ﬁnancial statemen ts are connuously evalua ted by the 
Compan y and are based on historical experience and various other assumpons and factors (including expect aons of 
futur e events) that the Compan y believ es to be reasonable under the exisng circums tances. Diﬀerences between 
actual results and esma tes are recognised in the period in which the results are known/ma terialised.
  The said esma tes are based on the facts and events, that existed as at the reporng date, or that occurr ed aer that date 
but provide addional evidence about condions exisng as at the reporng date.
 (c) Property , plan t and equipmen t 
  Tangible Asse ts
  Freehold land is carried at cost. All other items of property , plan t and equipmen t are stated at cost less depr eciaon and 
impairmen t, if any. Historical cost includes expenditur e that is directly aribut able to the acquision of the items.
  Leasehold land is stated at historical cost less amoun ts wri en oﬀ proporona te to expired lease period.
  Fixed Asse ts manuf actur ed / construct ed in house are valued at actual cost of raw materials, conversion cost, and other 
related cost, less accumula ted depr eciaon.st

  Depr eciaon methods, esma ted useful lives and residual value

 (o) Revenue recognion
  Revenue is recognised on the basis of appr oved contracts regarding the transfer of goods or services to a customer for an

bene ﬁts will ﬂow to the Compan y and the amoun t of income can be measur ed reliably).

 (t) Tax Expenses

 Gratuity Plan
 The Compan y provides for gratuity for employ ees in India as per the Paymen t of Gratuity Act, 1972. Employ ees who are in connuous service for

35 ­ Financial Risk Manag emen t
 Financial risk manag emen t objecv es and policies
 The Compan y’s ﬁnancial risk manag emen t is an integral part of how to plan and execut e its business strategies. The Compan y’s ﬁnancial risk 
manag emen t policy is set by the Managing Boar d.
 Mark et risk is the risk of loss of futur e earnings, fair values or futur e cash ﬂow s that may result from a chang e in the price of a ﬁnancial 
instrumen t. The value of a ﬁnancial instrumen t may chang e as a result of chang es in the interest rates, foreign currency exchang e rates, equity 
prices and other mark et chang es that aﬀect mark et risk sensiv e instrumen ts. Mark et risk is aribut able to all mark et risk sensiv e ﬁnancial 
instrumen ts including investmen ts and deposits, foreign currency receiv ables, payables and loans and borr owings.
 Mark et Risk­ Interest rate risk
 Interest rate risk is the risk that the fair value of futur e cash ﬂow s of the ﬁnancial instrumen ts will ﬂuctua te because of chang es in mark et 
interest rates. In order to opmiz e the Compan y’s posion with regards to interest income and interest expenses and to manag e the interest 
rate risk, treasury performs a compr ehensiv e corpor ate interest rate risk manag emen t by balancing the proporon of ﬁxed rate and ﬂoang 
rate ﬁnancial instrumen ts in its total por olio
Borr owing Bearing Fix ed rate of in terest 9,666.62 13,553.00
Borr owing Bearing v ariable r ate of in terest 60,342.60 61,321.69 As a t 31s t Mar ch, As a t 31s t Mar ch,
  2024 2023 Exposur e to interest rate risk
Mark et Risk­ Foreign currency risk
The Compan y oper ates interna onally and poron of the business is transact ed in several currencies. Consequen tly the Compan y is exposed to 
foreign exchang e risk through its sales and services in overseas and purchases from overseas supplier s in various foreign currencies. Exports of the 
compan y are signiﬁc antly lower in comparison to its imports. Foreign currency exchang e rate exposur e is partly balanced by exports of goods and 
pruden t hedging policy .

Credit risk
Credit risk arises from the possibility that the counter party may not be able to sele their oblig aons as agreed. To manag e this, the Compan y 
periodic ally assess ﬁnancial reliability of customer s, taking into account the ﬁnancial condion, current economic trends, and analy sis of historical 
bad debts and ageing of accounts receiv able. Individual risk limits are set accordingly .
The Compan y consider s the probability of default upon inial recognion of asset and whether there has been a signiﬁc ant increase in credit risk on 
an ongoing basis through each reporng period. To assess whether there is a signiﬁc ant increase in credit risk the Compan y compar es the risk of 
default occurring on asset as at the reporng date with the risk of default as at the date of inial recognion. It consider s reasonable and supporv e 
forward­looking informa on such as:
 i) Actual or expect ed signiﬁc ant adverse chang es in business
 ii) Actual or expect ed signiﬁc ant chang es in the oper ang results of the counterparty
 iii) Financial or economic condions that are expect ed to cause a signiﬁc ant chang e to the counterparty ’s ability to meet its oblig aons
 iv) Signiﬁc ant increase in credit risk on other ﬁnancial instrumen ts of the same counterparty
Liquidity Risk
Liquidity risk is deﬁned as the risk that the Compan y will not be able to sele or meet its oblig aons on me, or at a reasonable price. The Compan y’s 
treasury departmen t is responsible for liquidity , funding as well as selemen t manag emen t. In addion, processes and policies related such risk are 
overseen by senior manag emen t. Manag emen t monit ors the Compan y’s net liquidity posion through rolling forecasts on the basis of expect ed cash 
ﬂow s.

38 ­   Fair Value Measur emen t
 The fair values of the ﬁnancial assets and liabilies are included at the amoun t at which the instrumen t could be exchang ed in a current 
transacon betw een willing pares, other than in a forced or liquida on sale.
 The following methods and assumpons were used to esma te the fair values:
 • Fair value of cash and short ­term deposits, trade and other short term receiv ables, trade payables, other current liabilies, short term 
loans from bank s and other ﬁnancial instuons appr oxima te their carrying amoun ts largely due to short term maturies of these 
instrumen ts.
 • Financial instrumen ts with ﬁxed and variable interest rates are evalua ted by the Compan y based on paramet ers such as interest rates 
and individual credit worthiness of the counterparty . Based on this evalua on, allow ances are taken to account for expect ed losses of 
these receiv ables. Accordingly , fair value of such instrumen ts is not materially diﬀerent from their carrying amoun ts.Standalone

10. Our objecv es are to obtain reasonable assur ance about whe ther the consolida ted ﬁnancial statemen ts as a whole are free 
from material miss tatemen t, whe ther due to fraud or error, and to issue an audit or’s report that includes our opinion. 
Reasonable assur ance is a high level of assur ance, but is not a guar antee that an audit conduct ed in accordance with 
Standar ds on Auding will always detect a material miss tatemen t when it exists. Miss tatemen ts can arise from fraud or 
error and are consider ed material if, individually or in the aggregate, they could reasonably be expect ed to inﬂuence the 
economic decisions of user s taken on the basis of these consolida ted ﬁnancial statemen ts.

ANNEXURE A
Independen t Audit or’s Report on the Internal Financial Controls under Clause (i) of Sub­secon 3 of Secon 143 of the Companies 
Act, 2013 (the ‘Act’)
1. In conjuncon with our audit of the consolida ted ﬁnancial statemen ts of Time Technoplas t Limit ed ( the ‘Parent Compan y’), 
and its subsidiary (the Parent Compan y and its subsidiaries together referred to as the ‘Group’), and joint ventures as at and for 
the year ended 31 March 2024 we have audit ed the internal ﬁnancial controls over ﬁnancial reporng (IFCoFR) of the Parent 
Compan y, its subsidiary companies and its Joint venture companies, which are companies incorpor ated in India, as of that 
date.
Manag emen t’s Responsibility for Internal Financial Controls
2.  The respecv e Boar d of Directors of the Parent compan y, its subsidiary companies and  its joint venture companies, which are 
companies incorpor ated in India, are responsible for establishing and main taining internal ﬁnancial controls based on 
“internal control over ﬁnancial reporng criteria established by the Compan y considering the essen al componen ts of 
internal control stated in the Guidance Note on Audit of Internal Financial Controls Over Financial Reporng issued by the 
Instute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI)”. These responsibilies include the design, implementa on and 
main tenance of adequa te internal ﬁnancial controls that were oper ang eﬀecv ely for ensuring the orderly and eﬃcien t 
conduct of its business, including adher ence to the respecv e compan y’s policies, the safeguar ding of its asse ts, the 
prevenon and detecon of frauds and errors, the accur acy and comple teness of the accounng records, and the mely 
prepar aon of reliable ﬁnancial informa on, as requir ed under the Act.
Audit or’s Responsibility
3.  Our responsibility is to express an opinion on the I FCoFR the Parent Compan y, its subsidiary companies and its Joint venture 
companies as aforesaid, based on our audit. We conduct ed our audit in accordance with the Standar ds on Auding issued by 
the ICAI and deemed to be prescribed under Secon 143(10) of the Act, to the extent applic able to an audit of IFCoFR, and the 
Guidance Note issued by the ICAI. Those Standar ds and the Guidance Note requir e that we comply with ethical requir emen ts 
and plan and perform the audit to obtain reasonable assur ance about whe ther adequa te IFCoFR were established and 
main tained and if such controls oper ated eﬀecv ely in all material respects.
4.  Our audit involves performing procedur es to obtain audit evidence about the adequacy of IFCoFR and their oper ang 
eﬀecv eness. Our audit of IFCoFR includes obtaining an under standing of IFCoFR, assessing the risk that a material weakness 
exists, and t esng and ev alua ng the design and oper ang e ﬀecv eness of in ternal c ontrol based on the assessed risk. The 
procedur es select ed depend on the audit or’s judg emen t, including the assessmen t of the risks of material miss tatemen t of the 
ﬁnancial statemen ts, whe ther due to fraud or error.
5. We believ e that the audit evidence we have obtained and the audit evidence obtained by the other audit ors in terms of their 
reports referred to in the Other Maers paragraph below , is suﬃcien t and appr opria te to provide a basis for our audit opinion 
on the IFCoFR of the Parent Compan y, its subsidiary companies and its Joint venture companies as aforesaid.
Meaning of Internal Financial Controls Over Financial Reporng with reference to Consolida ted Financial Statemen ts
6.  A compan y’s IFCoFR is a process designed to provide reasonable assur ance regarding the reliability of ﬁnancial reporng and 
the prepar aon of ﬁnancial statemen ts for external purposes in accordance with gener ally accept ed accounng principles. A 
compan y’s IFCoFR includes those policies and procedur es that
 (1) pertain to the main tenance of records that, in reasonable detail, accur ately and fairly reﬂect the transacons and 
disposions of the asse ts of the compan y;

in accordance with gener ally accept ed accounng principles, and that receipts and expenditur es of the compan y are 
being made only in accordance with authorisa ons of manag emen t and directors of the  compan y; andConsolida ted

Note 1­STATEMENT OF SIGNIFICANT ACCOUNTING POLICIES:
A. Backgr ound
 Time Technoplas t Ltd  (TTL / the ‘Compan y’ or the parent compan y) , is subsidiaries and joint ventures  (the parent compan y 
and its subsidiaries and joint ventures together referred to as the “Group”) mainly involved in the manuf acturing of technology 
and innov aon driven polymer & Composit e products . The group has oper aons in local as well as in foreign countries. The 
Compan y is a public limit ed compan y and is listed on the Bomba y Stock Exchang e (BSE) and Naonal Stock Exchang e (NSE).
B. Signiﬁc ant Accoun ng Policies follow ed by the Compan y
 1. Basis of Prepar aon
  i) Compliance with Ind AS
   These consolida ted ﬁnancial statemen ts have been prepar ed in accordance with the Indian Accounng Standar ds 
(hereina er referred to as the ‘Ind AS’) as noﬁed by Minis try of Corpor ate Aﬀairs pursuan t to Secon 133 of the 
Companies Act, 2013 (‘Act’) read with of the Companies (Indian Accounng Standar ds) Rules,2015 as amended and 
other relevant provisions of the Act.
   The accounng policies are applied consis tently to all the periods presen ted in the ﬁnancial statemen ts. The Financial 
statemen ts of the Group has been consolida ted using uniform accounng policies
  ii) Historical cost convenon
   The ﬁnancial statemen ts have been prepar ed on a historical cost basis, except for the following which have been 
measur ed at fair value :
   1. Cert ain ﬁnancial asse ts and liabilies are measur ed at fair value;
   2. Deﬁned bene ﬁt plans;
   3. Equity seled Shar e Based Paymen ts 
  iii) Curr ent & non current classiﬁc aon
   All asse ts and liabilies have been classiﬁed as current or non­curr ent as per the Group’ s normal oper ang cycle 
(twelve mon ths) and other criteria set out in the Schedule III to the Act
  iv) Rounding of amoun ts
   All amoun ts disclosed in the ﬁnancial statemen ts and notes have been rounded oﬀ to the near est lakhs as per the 
requir emen t of  Schedule III, unless otherwise stated.
 2. Principle of Consolida on
  i) The ﬁnancial statemen ts of the parent Compan y and its subsidiaries are combined on a line by line basis by adding 
together like items of asse ts, liabilies, equity , incomes, expenses and cash ﬂow s, aer fully elimina ng intra­group 
balances and intra­group transacons. 
  ii) Proﬁts or losses resulng from intra­group transacons that are recognised in asse ts, such as inventory and 
property , plan t & equipmen t, are elimina ted in full. 
  iii) In case of foreign subsidiaries, revenue items are consolida ted at the average rate prevailing during the year. All 
asse ts and liabilies are converted at rates prevailing at the end of the year. Any exchang e diﬀerence arising on 
consolida on is recognised in the Foreign Curr ency Transla on Reser ve. 
  iv) The audit ed /unaudit ed ﬁnancial statemen ts of foreign Subsidiaries /join t ventures have been prepar ed in 
accordance with the gener ally accept ed accounng principle of its country of incorpor aon or Ind AS. 
  v) The diﬀerence in accounng policies of the parent compan y and its subsidiaries are not material and there are no 
material transacon from 1st January 2024 to 31st March 2024 in respect of subsidiaries having ﬁnancial year ended 
31st December 2023
  vi) The consolida ted ﬁnancial statemen t have been prepar ed using uniform accounng policies for like transacon and 
other events in similar circums tances. st

 3. Summary of Signiﬁc ant Accoun ng Policies

Diﬀerences between actual results and esmates are recognised in the period in which the results are

   The said esma tes are based on the facts and events, that existed as at the reporng date, or that occurr ed aer that

  b. Property , plan t and equipmen t

for their intended use at the reporng date
   Depr eciaon methods, esma ted useful lives and residual value

  d. Lease
   The Group, as a lessee, recognises a right­of­use asse t and a lease liability for its leasing arrangemen ts, if the contract 
conveys the right to control the use of an iden ﬁed asse t. 
   The contract conveys the right to control the use of an iden ﬁed asse t, if it involves the use of an iden ﬁed asse t and 
the group has subs tanally all of the economic bene ﬁts from use of the asse t and has right to direct the use of the 
iden ﬁed asse t. The cost of the right­of­use asse t shall comprise of the amoun t of the inial measur emen t of the lease 
liability adjus ted for any lease paymen ts made at or before the commencemen t date plus any inial direct costs 
incurr ed. The right­of­use asse ts is subsequen tly measur ed at cost less any accumula ted depr eciaon, accumula ted 
impairmen t losses, if any and adjus ted for any remeasur emen t of the lease liability . The right­of­use asse ts is 
depr eciated using the straigh t­line method from the commencemen t date over the short er of lease term or useful life 
of right­of­use asse t. 
   The Group measur es the lease liability at the presen t value of the lease paymen ts that are not paid at the 
commencemen t date of the lease. The lease paymen ts are discounted using the interest rate implicit in the lease, if 
that rate can be readily determined. If that rate cannot be readily determined, the Group uses incremen tal borr owing 
rate. 
   For short ­term and low value leases, the Group recognises the lease paymen ts as an oper ang expense on a straigh t­
line basis over the lease term

  h. Impairmen t of  non­ ﬁnancial asse ts
   The Group assesses at each reporng date whe ther there is any objecv e evidence that a non­ ﬁnancial asse t or a

asse ts is consider ed as a cash gener ang unit. If any such indic aon exists, an esma te of the recoverable amoun t of 
the individual asse t/cash gener ang unit is made.
   An impairmen t loss is calcula ted as the diﬀerence between an asse t’s carrying amoun t and recoverable amoun t. 
Losses are recognised in proﬁt or loss. When the Group consider s that there are no realis c prospects of recovery of 
the asse t, the relevant amoun ts are wri en oﬀ. If the amoun t of impairmen t loss subsequen tly decr eases and the 
decr ease can be related objecv ely to an event occurring aer the impairmen t was recogniz ed, then the previously 
recogniz ed impairmen t loss is reversed through proﬁt or loss.

  r. Tax Expenses

   Deferred tax

ﬁnancial statemen ts and the corresponding tax bases used in the comput aon of taxable proﬁt. 
   Deferred tax liabilies and asse ts are measur ed at the tax rates that are expect ed to apply in the period in which the 
liability is seled or the asse t realised, based on tax rates (and tax laws) that have been enact ed or subs tanvely 
enact ed by the end of the reporng period. The carrying amoun t of Deferred tax liabilies and asse ts are review ed at 
the end of each reporng period.

33 ­ Financial Risk Manag emen t
 Financial risk manag emen t objecv es and policies
 The Groups and its Joint venture Compan y's ﬁnancial risk manag emen t is an integral part of how to plan and execut e its business strategies. 
The Groups ﬁnancial risk manag emen t policy is set by the Managing Boar d.
 Mark et risk is the risk of loss of futur e earnings, fair values or futur e cash ﬂow s that may result from a chang e in the price of a ﬁnancial 
instrumen t. The value of a ﬁnancial instrumen t may chang e as a result of chang es in the interest rates, foreign currency exchang e rates, equity 
prices and other mark et chang es that aﬀect mark et risk sensiv e instrumen ts. Mark et risk is aribut able to all mark et risk sensiv e ﬁnancial 
instrumen ts including investmen ts and deposits, foreign currency receiv ables, payables and loans and borr owings.st

 Mark et Risk­ Interest rate risk
 Interest rate risk is the risk that the fair value of futur e cash ﬂow s of the ﬁnancial instrumen ts will ﬂuctua te because of chang es in mark et 
interest rates. In order to opmiz e the groups posion with regards to interest income and interest expenses and to manag e the interest rate 
risk, treasury performs a compr ehensiv e corpor ate interest rate risk manag emen t by balancing the proporon of ﬁxed rate and ﬂoang rate 
ﬁnancial instrumen ts in its total por oliost

Mark et Risk­ Foreign currency risk
The group oper ates interna onally and poron of the business is transact ed in several currencies. Consequen tly the Compan y is exposed to 
foreign exchang e risk through its sales and services in overseas and purchases from overseas supplier s in various foreign currencies. Exports of 
the compan y are signiﬁc antly lower in comparison to its imports. Foreign currency exchang e rate exposur e is partly balanced by exports of 
goods and pruden t hedging policy .

Credit risk
Credit risk arises fr om the possibility tha t the c ounter party ma y not be able t o sele their oblig aons as agr eed. T o manag e this, the gr oup 
periodic ally assess ﬁnancial reliability of customer s, taking into account the ﬁnancial condion, current economic trends, and analy sis of 
historical bad debts and ageing of accounts receiv able. Individual risk limits are set accordingly .
The group consider s the probability of default upon inial recognion of asset and whether there has been a signiﬁc ant increase in credit risk 
on an ongoing basis through each reporng period. To assess whether there is a signiﬁc ant increase in credit risk the Compan y compar es the 
risk of default occurring on asset as at the reporng date with the risk of default as at the date of inial recognion. It consider s reasonable and 
supporv e forward­looking informa on such as:
 i) Actual or expect ed signiﬁc ant adverse chang es in business
 ii) Actual or expect ed signiﬁc ant chang es in the oper ang results of the counterparty
 iii) Financial or economic condions that are expect ed to cause a signiﬁc ant chang e to the counterparty ’s ability to meet its 
oblig aons
 iv) Signiﬁc ant increase in credit risk on other ﬁnancial instrumen ts of the same counterparty
Liquidity Risk
Liquidity risk is deﬁned as the risk that the group will not be able to sele or meet its oblig aons on me, or at a reasonable price. The 
Compan y’s treasury departmen t is responsible for liquidity , funding as well as selemen t manag emen t. In addion, processes and policies 
related such risk are overseen by senior manag emen t. Manag emen t monit ors the groups net liquidity posion through rolling forecasts on the 
basis of expect ed cash ﬂow s.TIME TECHNOPLAST LIMITED Consolida ted

 36 ­ Fair Value Measur emen t
 The fair values of the ﬁnancial assets and liabilies are included at the amoun t at which the instrumen t could be exchang ed in a current 
transacon betw een willing pares, other than in a forced or liquida on sale.
 The following methods and assumpons were used to esma te the fair values:
 • Fair value of cash and short ­term deposits, trade and other short term receiv ables, trade payables, other current liabilies, short term 
loans from bank s and other ﬁnancial instuons appr oxima te their carrying amoun ts largely due to short term maturies of these 
instrumen ts.
 • Financial instrumen ts with ﬁxed and variable interest rates are evalua ted by the Compan y based on paramet ers such as interest rates 
and individual credit worthiness of the counterparty . Based on this  evalua on, allow ances are taken to account for expect ed losses of 
these receiv ables. Accordingly , fair value of such instrumen ts is not materially diﬀerent from their carrying amoun ts.
